The within-seed Jaccard of 0.27 versus 0.10 cross-architecture indicates that models from the same architecture naturally show much greater agreement on which examples benefit or suffer from fine-tuning, which could confound the entrainment signal I'm trying to detect. The script's logic is checking whether within-seed overlap is significantly higher than cross-architecture overlap—if it is, that's marked as \\\"BAD\\\" because it suggests the entrainment effect isn't transferring across architectures. The opposite case, where cross-architecture overlap exceeds within-seed overlap, would be \\\"STRONG SUPPORT\\\" for entrainment. So the 0.27 vs 0.10 result actually makes intuitive sense: same-architecture models should naturally be more similar than different-architecture ones, which doesn't necessarily contradict the entrainment hypothesis.
